# P28 progress: Codex-style peter() console

Plan: `dev/plan/P28-console-codex.md`. One section per task (conventions section 11). The research scratch is
not kept, so the facts the plan relies on are recorded first.

## Evidence (research, 2026-10-21)

Sources: Codex source at tag rust-v0.157.1 (commit 36650394c5b3); Codex 0.157.1 driven live in a pty with a VT
emulator (runs 1-7); R terminal tooling probed on CRAN macOS R 4.6.1 (libedit "readline 5.2") in tmux 3.8, with
cli 3.6.6 and the RStudio 2026.09 and Ark sources; a gptr audit (pty harness, scenarios A-I, drain, history and
composer checks); two independent judges (scores 7 and 7).

### Audit scenarios A-I (gptr 8d9a388)
Harness: terminal R in a processx pty, 80 columns, TERM=xterm-256color, `peter(mode = "manual")` on a fake
provider. Request 1 streams about 2.5 s of text, then calls r `zz = 1`, which asks (E and F: request 1 runs a
read-only tool that blocks R for 3 s, request 2 asks; H: request 1 asks an ask-tool question). Later requests
echo `REQ<n> roles=...`. Every ask showed the call twice: `● r  zz = 1`, then `r  zz = 1` above the question.

| Scenario | What was typed | Key screen line | REQ roles | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | `steer me` + Enter while streaming | `[gptr] steering: 'steer me'; it reaches the agent at its next step` | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result,operator` (the relay) | works |
| B | `use base R`, no Enter, then the ask | `allow? [y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]: use base R`; Enter gives `Not an answer: 'use base R'. Type y, a, n (n <text> says what to do instead) o` / `r ? for details.` | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result` | text lost, mid-word wrap |
| C | `no wait, plot it first`, no Enter | `allow? ...: no wait, plot it first`; Enter gives `error: Permission denied: wait, plot it first. ...` | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result`, last = the denial | becomes a denial |
| D | `steer me` + Enter just as the ask is drawn | `allow? ...: steer me`, then the question again | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result` | line discarded (stale rule) |
| E | `steer me` + Enter while the tool blocks R | the steering notice after `done (3.0 s)` | REQ3 `user,assistant,tool_result,operator,assistant,tool_result` | reached REQ2, by timing (Task 7 makes it certain) |
| F | `and also`, no Enter, while the tool blocks R | `allow? ...: and also`; `Not an answer: 'and also'. ...` runs to column 80, the rest at column 0 | REQ3 `user,assistant,tool_result,assistant,tool_result` | text lost |
| G | `use base R`, no Enter, then Ctrl-C | `[gptr] paused sc762fa45e0: [s]teer, [f]ollow-up, [c]ontinue, [a]bort,`; the menu's `? ` starts empty | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result,operator` after retyping at `steer>` | draft lost (SIGINT flush) |
| H | `use base R`, no Enter, at the ask-tool question | `Choose (a number; or type your own answer): use base R` | REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result`, last `The user answered: - q1: (typed) use base R` | becomes the answer |
| I | `use base R`, no Enter, as the run ends | `peter> use base R` at the next prompt; Enter sends it | REQ2 `user,assistant,user`, last `use base R` | works |

Root cause of B, C, F and H: during a run the terminal is canonical with echo off and the partial line waits in
the kernel buffer; the question's `readline()` leaves canonical mode, inherits it, shows it after the prompt and
takes it as the answer.

### Codex 0.157.1, driven live (runs 3-6)
- Steer and queue preview: Enter while working put `↳ also count the lines in each file` (dim) under the working
  row, below a line ending `(press esc to interrupt and send immediately)`. Tab queued
  `↳ then reply with one word: done` (dim italic) with `⌥+↑ edit last queued message`. At delivery a queued
  message became a user cell `› then reply with one word: done` on a shaded band (#404040; the composer band was
  #373737), and the composer kept its draft (`draft not sent`) through the turn and the approval.
- Approval overlay: `Would you like to run the following command?`, `Environment: local`, `Reason: ...`,
  `$ touch approved.txt`, then `› 1. Yes, proceed (y)`, `2. Yes, and don't ask again for commands that start with
  ... (p)`, `3. No, and tell Codex what to do differently (esc)` and `Press enter to confirm or esc to cancel`.
- The `yes` fall-through: at the overlay `zq` was ignored, then `yes` approved on `y` (`approved ... touch
  approved.txt this time`) and `es` was appended to the restored draft (`my unsent draftes`).
- Trigger-only popups: `@al` and `look at @be` (a word start mid-sentence) open the file and plugin popup, `$` the
  skills popup, `/` the command popup; a leading `!` turns the glyph into a red `!` with `Shell mode`. Natural
  language (`please read alpha.txt and tell me what it sais`), `email me at wan@` and `cost is $5 and x$y` open
  nothing.

### Measured facts
- cli 3.6.6 colour mapping (re-measured in Task 1, R 4.6.1), `cli::make_ansi_style(hex)` at 256 / 16 and 8 /
  truecolor: `#b85450` gives `38;5;167` / `31` / `38;2;184;84;80`; `#557ea8` gives `38;5;67` / `90` /
  `38;2;85;126;168`; `#6c8ebf` and `#5f87af` both give `38;5;110` / `90`. cli's `"red"` and `"blue"` at 16 colours
  are `31` and `34`. WCAG contrast on #1c1c1c and on white: #b85450 3.59 and 4.75, cell 167 4.62 and 3.69, #557ea8
  4.01 and 4.25, cell 67 4.52 and 3.77, cell 110 7.42 and 2.30.
- Prompt markers on libedit (tmux 3.8, CRAN macOS R 4.6.1, 60 columns): with the prompt
  `\001\033[48;5;237m\002peter> \001\033[K\002` and `\033[0m\033[K` written after each read, the band filled to
  column 60 while typing and after Backspace edits, the submitted row stayed shaded in scrollback and the next
  line was clean. A wrapped input's second row is shaded only under its text. GNU readline 8.x is not yet run.
- Drain check (macOS BSD tty; Linux n_tty reasoned, not run): with echo off and canonical mode, `done line` Enter
  `use base R` typed during a 3 s sleep. Without a drain the question's `readline()` returned `done line` and
  `use base R` leaked to the shell after exit. With `stty -icanon min 0 time 0`, a `processx::conn_create_fd(0L,
  close = FALSE)` read (50 ms polls, `conn_read_chars()`) until empty, then the saved modes, the drain got
  `done line\nuse base R` and the question started empty (`y` answered).
- History check: `utils::timestamp(stamp = <text>, prefix = "", suffix = "", quiet = TRUE)` adds a line to
  readline's history; Up at the next `peter> ` recalled it and the person appended to it.
- Composer prototype: non-canonical, no echo, `min 0 time 0`; whole transcript rows committed as `\r\033[K` + row
  + `\n` above one redrawn bottom row. The draft showed on that row while text streamed; Enter queued it
  (`↳ use base R`); `and plot itx` then DEL left the draft `and plot it`. (Its Braille frame is not adopted.)
- Completion prototype (scoped `utils::rc.options(custom.completer = f)` at `readline("peter> ")`, libedit):
  `Compare @mtc` gives `@mtcars_copy`, `!my_lo` gives `my_long_object_name`, `!!summary(mtcars_c` gives
  `mtcars_copy`, `/mo` gives `/mode`, `see @analysis_n` gives `@analysis_notes.R`, and `summarise the da` stays
  unchanged. R's default completion instead turned `summarise the da` into `summarise the dat` and listed R
  functions (`/model.frame`, `/mode<-`, `/months.Date`, ...) for `/mo`. A completer that leaves `env$fileName`
  unset aborts the `readline()` call.
- OSC 11 and DA1 in detached tmux: no OSC 11 reply, only DA1 (research: 28 ms; re-measured in Task 1 with tmux
  3.8: `\033[?1;2;4c` after 0.008 s), so the background is unknown there and no band is drawn.

## Task 2 - A question never takes typed-ahead text (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-typing$'`, the new tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 15 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 100 ]`.
  `console_typed_split()` was not found. In the drain test the history mock got nothing, and REQ2's last message was
  `+ zz <numeric length 1>`, not the relay of `steer me`. In the pty test B's prompt read
  `[y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]: use base R`, and the later steps cascaded (`Not an answer: 'C'`, C's prompt
  ending `[?]: no wait, plot it first`, H's not ending `your own answer): `). The counts of
  `kept your unfinished line`, `Not an answer`, `Permission denied` and `(typed)` were `0 3 2 1`, against
  `3 0 0 0`.
- Green: `console-typing` `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 118 ]` (36 s for the file). B, C and H run as three
  prompts of one pty child (80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, echo on), so R starts once:
  - B: the approval row ends with `[?]: `, `y` alone approves, and the notice comes before the question. At the next
    prompt Up then Enter sends `use base R` (`REQ user: use base R END`): the history recall works.
  - C: `no wait, plot it first` denies nothing, and REQ shows the tool's result.
  - H: the ask prompt ends with `your own answer): `, and `1` gives `The user answered: - q1: ggplot2`.
  - A mock test checks the stty order `-g`, `-echo`, `-g`, `-icanon min 0 time 0`, `SAVED`, `SAVED`: the drain's modes
    come back before the question reads.
- pty check: the audit harness reran scenarios B, C, F and H on this code (80 columns, macOS libedit). The screen
  shows `  [gptr] kept your unfinished line; ↑ recalls it at the next prompt` above an empty
  `  allow? [y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]:` (B, C, F) and above an empty
  `  Choose (a number; or type your own answer):` (H). In B, `y` then gave REQ2 `user,assistant,tool_result` with
  last `[+ zz <numeric length 1>]`; in F it gave REQ3 `user,assistant,tool_result,assistant,tool_result`. The
  scenarios' scripted Enter at the now-empty prompt gives `Not an answer: ''`. Answer wrapping is Task 3's job.
  C and H then time out, because the script never gives an answer.
- Linux n_tty: a docker `python:3.12-slim` container (local image, `--network none`, kernel 6.12.76-linuxkit)
  ran a pty with gptr's stty sequence. `done line\nuse base R` was typed with echo off in canonical mode. After
  `stty -icanon min 0 time 0` a read got `done line\nuse base R`. After the saved modes came back, `read` got `y`
  alone (`ANSWER[y]`). The gptr pty test itself has not run on Linux: there is no Linux R library here, and
  nothing was downloaded. The ubuntu NOT_CRAN lane must pass it before Task 19 builds on the drain.
- Also: the existing test "console_typed_lines() reads what fd 0 holds ..." (test-console-repl.R) now expects
  `structure("half typed", partial = TRUE)`. Complete reads carry no attribute. The fd-0 child of "the fd-0 reader
  returns complete lines ..." defines `console_typed_split()`. Spec changes: 04 §7.1 gets a `tty_drain_mode()` row,
  §15.10 gets IC-76's Task 2 text, 03 §6.17 gets the drain sentence, and D-243 gets its tests so far.
  `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`: `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 5 files, no lints. Neighbours (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|
  session-object)`): FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 3923.
- Deviations: none. The kept line goes to the history whatever `gptr.history` says: that is the plan's rule, and the
  notice promises the recall. The notice text is the plan's and does not repeat the line. Open: the pty cases
  on the Linux lane.

## Task 3 - A line typed before a question is queued, never dropped (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-(ui|typing)$'`, the final tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 15 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 242 ]`.
  `answer_stale` was not found. During a run the stale `use base R` was not queued (`queued` empty) and REQ2's last
  message was `+ zz <numeric length 1>`, not the relay. The paste notice still read `(3 lines dropped)` and the
  history mock got nothing. The invalid-answer notice read `Not an answer: 'zz'. Type y, a, n (n <text> says what
  to do instead) or ? for details.`, kept nothing and was one 120-column row at width 60. In pty D the child printed
  `DRAWN` (the line arrived after the drain), but no `[gptr] steering: 'steer me'` and no relay followed. `Not an
  answer` was not seen: the line was discarded.
- Green: `console-ui` and `arch-layers` `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 179 ]`; `console-typing`
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 127 ]` (41 s for the file).
  - The stale case outside a run keeps `h$config$codex` and `y` in the history (the blank line is dropped) and reads 4
    times. During a run (REPL, manual mode) it gives one queue item `list(text = "use base R", as = "steer",
    source = "repl")`, two approval reads and REQ2 ending with the relay. A secret input keeps nothing.
  - The history mock gets the paste as `y\npasted line two\npasted line three` and each non-empty invalid answer.
    The notices are `  A pasted block is not an answer (kept in history).` and `  Not an answer: 'zz' (kept in
    history). Type y, a, p, n or ?.`. They read `Not an answer: ''. Type y, n or ?.` for an empty answer and drop
    ` (kept in history)` where R is not interactive (a piped `.stdin` session).
  - At width 60 the 40-character answer wraps as `  Not an answer: 'please use base R graphics for this plot'` /
    `  (kept in history). Type y, a, p, n or ?.`.
  - pty D (80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, echo on): the child traces `ui_permission_lines()` to print `DRAWN` and
    sleep 1 s, so `steer me` + Enter arrives after the drain and before the question's readline(). The screen
    shows `allow? [y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]: steer me`, then
    `[gptr] steering: 'steer me'; it reaches the agent at its next step`, then the question again, empty. `y` then
    gives `REQ operator: The user sent this message while you were working: steer me` (wrapped before ` END` at
    80 columns, so the test does not match it).
- pty check: in a hand-driven run of D, a `y` sent at once after the re-asked prompt was itself stale and was
  queued (`[gptr] steering: 'y'`). That is the rule, not a fault: a person answers more than 0.1 s after a prompt
  shows. The test waits 0.5 s (`pending()`), as Task 2's cases do. A second run, without the trace, typed the
  40-character answer at the approval. At 80 columns the notice was
  `  Not an answer: 'please use base R graphics for this plot' (kept in history).` / `  Type y, a, p, n or ?.`,
  then `y` approved (REQ `tool_result`).
- Plan amendment (same commit): the plan has `readline_answer()` (utils-options.R, L0) call `policy_enqueue()`.
  `arch-layers` rejects that: `readline_answer (utils-options.R) -> policy_enqueue (console-interrupt.R)`, and the
  same for `policy_find`, `policy_targets` and `console_history_add`. L0 reaches upper layers only through their
  callbacks (03 §2.2). So `readline_answer(prompt, keep = console_history_add)` hands each line it reads past to
  `keep`:
  - the console questions (`ui_console_read()`, `console_ask()`) pass `console_typed_ahead()` (console-interrupt.R):
    the run's session through `policy_enqueue(..., source = "repl")`, else the history;
  - `gptr_confirm()` keeps the default, the history;
  - a secret input passes NULL and keeps nothing (also not its paste).
  `console_history_add()` moved from console-repl.R to utils-options.R. The plan's Task 3 decisions, Files line and
  the File Structure row of console-interrupt.R say so.
- Also: the 100th read is returned and never also queued. The shared `local_readline()` helper and `console_try()`
  record history adds (`kept`), so a test never writes the developer's history. Task 2's drain test now reads
  `rl$kept`, and a `readline_answer` mock takes `...`. Spec: 04 §7.1 row (`readline_answer(prompt, keep)`,
  `answer_stale(secs)`, `console_history_add(x)`), §15.10 IC-75, 03 §6.17, D-243's Task 3 note. `?peter` and the
  interactive-console vignette (`.Rmd` and `.Rmd.orig`) now say that lines typed before a question are queued
  during a run, else go to the history, and that a paste at a question goes to the history.
  `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (dev library on R_LIBS_USER): `All four plan checks passed`, with
  `990 findings (info 826, warn 164)` against HEAD's `986 (info 826, warn 160)`. All four new findings are
  `names X but no plan defines it` warnings (`answer_stale`, `console_typed_ahead`, `console_wrap`, the new
  `readline_answer` signature).
- Lint: 7 files, no lints. Neighbours with arch and utils
  (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|session-object|arch-layers|utils-options)`):
  FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4071.
- Deviations: the layering amendment above. The invalid-answer notice keeps the plan's text. It drops
  ` (kept in history)` when nothing was kept, for an empty answer or outside an interactive R. Open: none.

## Task 4 - Every queued steer reaches the next step boundary (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^agent-run$'`, the new tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 3 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 668 ]`. Two steers
  enqueued by one tool took 3 requests, not 2: REQ2's roles ended `tool_result,operator` (one relay) and the
  second relay, `... working: and log2`, waited for REQ3.
- Pinning tests, green before and after: a steer enqueued at the final answer's `message_end` adds one request
  to the same run (REQ2 `user,assistant,operator`, `s$turns` 1); a steer from the first of two calls reaches REQ2
  after both results (`user,assistant,tool_result,tool_result,operator`) and the second call ran; two relays after
  the results serialise in each native adapter's body: anthropic one `system` message holding both texts,
  openai-completions two `user` messages after `tool`, openai-responses two `developer` items after

  `test '^provider-transform$'` gave `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 139 ]`.
- Green: `run_take(run, "steer")` takes the whole FIFO in order, each item its own `queue_item_message()`;
  follow-ups are still taken one at a time, and one `queue_update` is emitted per take.
  `test '^(agent-run|agent-loop|provider-transform)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 944 ]`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): the change turned report 02's oracles L11-L13 red
  (`[ FAIL 5 | ... | PASS 937 ]`: L11's roles had one `assistant` less, L12's `STEER-2` and `FOLLOWUP` moved up by
  one message, L13 saw 3 requests). They pinned P06's adaptation "gptr has no 'all' mode". They now expect both
  relays in REQ2 (L11 `user,assistant,tool_result,operator,operator,assistant,user,assistant`; L12 each relay holds

  changes L12 to adapted and L13 to port. The plan's Task 4 now names them, and the File Structure row adds

- Also: `run_initial_input()` uses the same take, so a run started without input opens with every queued steer.

## Task 5 - queue_update names the item; session_unqueue() takes one back (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^(session-object|agent-run)$'`, the new tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 4 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 |
  PASS 1188 ]`. The add events had only `steer` and `follow_up` (`op`, `which`, `text` and `source` were NULL), no
  event had `op = "take"` (length 0 against 3), and `session_unqueue()` was not found (two errors).
- Green: `queue_emit(s, op, which, item)` (session-object.R) emits `queue_update` with `op`, `which`, the item's
  redacted `text` and `source`, then the counts the queue holds at that moment. `session_enqueue()` emits `add`.
  `run_take()` still removes its items at once, then emits one `take` per item, in order, so a boundary that takes
  two steers emits two events, each with the counts left (`steer = 0` for both). `session_unqueue(s, which =
  "steer")` removes the newest `which` item whose source is in `queue_user_sources`, emits `withdraw` and returns
  the item (`text`, `blocks`, `source`, `t`), else NULL without an event. `test '^(session-object|agent-run)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 1202 ]`; `test '^(ext-events|arch-layers|console-jsonl|session-store)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 551 ]`.
  - Add: `use sk-ant-api03-...` as a repl steer gives `op = "add", which = "steer", text = "use
    [secret:anthropic-key]", source = "repl", steer = 1, follow_up = 0`.
  - Take: an idle session with the follow-up `go` (api_user), run with no input, whose tool enqueues `use TPM`
    (pipe) and `and log2` (repl): three takes, `go` (follow_up), then `use TPM` and `and log2` (steer), all with
    counts 0 and 0.
  - Withdraw: from steers pipe, repl, extension, agent and a repl follow-up, the first call returns `and log2`
    (counts 3 and 1 after), the second `use TPM`, the third NULL with `extension` and `agent` still queued;
    `"follow_up"` returns `then plot`; 8 events in all; `which = "later"` is `gptr_error_invalid_argument`. A fresh
    session returns NULL for both kinds and emits nothing.
- Also: the `ext-events.R` catalogue row reads `op, which, text, source, steer, follow_up`. Spec: the 04 §10.4
  `queue_update` row (one event per item added, taken by the run or withdrawn), a 04 §7.6 row for
  `session_unqueue()`, one sentence in 03 §6.2 and D-242's Task 5 note. The abort path still moves the queue to
  `dropped` without an event; Task 15 owns it. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`: `All four plan checks
  passed`, with `991 findings (info 826, warn 165)` against HEAD's 990: the new one is
  `contract_fn_undefined ... 04 names session_unqueue(s, which = "steer") ... but no plan defines it`, as for
  Task 3's new names.
- pty or live check: none. The change is in the session queue, below the console; Tasks 13, 15 and 18 consume it.
- Lint: 5 files, no lints. Neighbours with the catalogue and layering
  (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|session-object|ext-events|arch-layers)`): FAIL 0,

## Task 6 - Permission requests carry the tool call id (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^agent-dispatch$'`, the new test on the old code): `[ FAIL 1 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 216 ]`. The
  UI saw two requests (a direct `outer`, then its nested `inner`) and both had `tool_call_id` NULL, against
  `c("c_outer", "c_outer/1")`.
- Green: `perm_request_record()` adds `tool_call_id = call$id`. A direct call carries its block id; a nested member

## Task 7 - The step boundary reads typed input before steers are polled (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-typing$'`, the test on the old code): `[ FAIL 2 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 125 ]`. The test
  "a line typed during a tool reaches the request right after its result" now keeps the periodic typing task from
  ever ticking (a `reactor_task` mock returns an id and registers nothing for `console_typed`). Its slow in-process
  tool sleeps 0.25 s, then fd 0 yields `and also use mtcars`. REQ2's roles ended `assistant,tool_result`, against
  `tool_result,operator`, and its last message was `slept`, not the relay. There were two requests, so the line
  never reached the run. Before this task the same test passed only because the 0.1 s task happened to tick
  between the tool's end and the steer poll.
- Green: `console_on_turn_end()` (console-render.R) runs `console_typed()` and `policy_flush()`, and
  `console_hooks()` registers it on `turn_end`. after_turn emits `turn_end` just before it polls steers. Now
  `console_typed()` checks `repl_terminal()` itself, so it reads nothing in a `.stdin`, jsonl or IDE-console session.
  `console_before_message()` therefore calls it for a prompt without its own check.
  `test '^console-typing$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 127 ]`. "no fd-0 reads outside a terminal REPL
  (.stdin, jsonl, peter() with a prompt)" still counts 0 reads.
- pty check (scenario E; terminal R in a processx pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, temporary HOME, R_USER_* and
  GPTR_PROJECT_ROOT; scratch driver, not kept). `peter(mode = "manual")` runs on a fake provider whose first answer
  calls a read-only tool that blocks R for 3 s. `steer me` + Enter is typed 1 s into it. A `trace()` on
  `reactor_task` swaps the typing task for one that returns FALSE at its first tick. On this code the screen shows
  `done (3.0 s)`, then `[gptr] steering: 'steer me'; it reaches the agent at its next step`, then
  `REQ2 roles=user,assistant,tool_result,operator END`. On HEAD (db8bf23, from `git archive`) REQ2 reads
  `roles=user,assistant,tool_result END`, and `steer me` waits for the next `peter> ` prompt, where it joins
  `/exit` as a paste. Without the trace, this code gives the same REQ2 relay.
- Also: spec 04 section 7.14 (the `builtin_console` row lists the `turn_end` hook) and D-242's Task 7 note.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 7's Files line names `R/console-repl.R` (`console_typed()`), and a decision
  says it checks `repl_terminal()`. The File Structure row of console-repl.R adds Task 7. The check has to live
  somewhere, or the hook would read a `.stdin` session's reader. One check in the callee replaces the copy in
  `console_before_message()`. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`: `All four plan checks passed`, with
  `991 findings (info 826, warn 165)`, as at HEAD.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|session-object)`):
  FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 3980. With the layering and extension tests
  (`...|arch-layers|ext-)`): FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 5886. Full suite (`test '.'`):
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 64 | PASS 35090 ]`.
- Deviations: the amendment above. Open: none.

## Review fixes of Tasks 1-7 (2026-10-21)
A review of Tasks 1-7 confirmed 12 findings. Each fix below is its own commit; none is rejected.

### Fix 1 - A history entry of several lines comes back as one prompt (Tasks 2-3)
- Finding: Task 3 keeps a block pasted at a question as one history entry with embedded newlines. In the pty
  (80x24, libedit), Up then Enter at the next `peter> ` sent each line as its own prompt, so the first one sent
  the approval answer `y` to the agent. A block pasted at `peter> ` had the same problem.
- Red (`test '^console-(repl|typing)$'`, the new tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 5 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 355 ]`.
  `console_history_add()` passed `y\npasted two` and `!f = function() {\n1\n}` to `utils::timestamp()` unwrapped,
  and the REPL read them back as `y`, then `pasted two`. The new pty case (macOS only: paste joining reads a macOS
  terminal) timed out waiting for `REQ user: y pasted two pasted three END`. With its prompt-paste step added, the
  screen showed 7 `REQ user: ` answers against 3, `REQ user: y END` among them.
- Green: `console_history_add()` adds text of several lines that is not a `"""` block or a fence as a `"""` block.
  The REPL's grammar reads it back as one input, and a block whose body starts with `!` or `/` is still dispatched
  as code or a command. `test '^console-(repl|typing)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 360 ]`.
- pty check (the new case "a macOS terminal: a block pasted at a question comes back as one prompt"):
  `y` / `pasted two` / `pasted three` pasted at the approval gives `A pasted block is not an answer (kept in
  history).`, `n` denies, and Up then Enter at `peter> ` gives one request, `REQ user: y pasted two pasted three
  END`. `a` / `b` / `c` pasted at `peter> ` and sent gives `REQ user: a b c END`; Up then Enter sends it once more,
  as one request. On the old code the same steps gave 7 requests.
- Also: 04 §7.1 (`console_history_add()`), §15.10 IC-75 and D-243's review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`:
  `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|
  session-object)`): FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 3998.
- Deviations: none.

### Fix 2 - The kept-line notice quotes the line and names the Up key (Task 2)
- Findings: Task 2's `[gptr] kept your unfinished line; ↑ recalls it at the next prompt` never showed the text,
  though the person typed it with the echo off, and every other queue notice quotes its text. The promise "at the
  next prompt" was false when Enter landed on the approval: the end of the sentence became a second history entry
  after it. Under LANG=C, cli's ASCII arrow `^` read as a Ctrl key.
- Red (`test '^console-typing$'`, the new expectations on the old code): `[ FAIL 4 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 138 ]`.
  The drain test found no `[gptr] kept in history (Up): 'use base R'` row. Pty B timed out waiting for
  `[gptr] kept in history (` and `): 'use base R'`, and the B, C, H counts were `0 0 0 0` against `3 0 0 0`.
- Green: the notice is `[gptr] kept in history (<up>): '<line>'`, the line escaped, Task 15's form.
  `console_symbols()` gains `up`: `↑` (U+2191) where the output is UTF-8, else `Up`.
  `test '^console-typing$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 143 ]`.
- pty check (scratch driver, not kept: terminal R in a processx pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, echo on;
  scenario B, `use base R` typed without Enter while the answer streams). The screen shows
  `  [gptr] kept in history (↑): 'use base R'` above `  allow? [y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]: y`. With LANG=C and
  LC_ALL=C it shows `  [gptr] kept in history (Up): 'use base R'`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 2's notice text, and Task 8's glyph table gains the Up key (`↑`, `Up`), so
  Tasks 15 and 17 share it. Also: 04 §15.10 IC-76 and D-243's review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`:
  `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 3999.
- Deviations: the amendment above.

### Fix 3 - gptr.history decides every console history add (Tasks 2-3; D-243 note)
- Finding: `gptr.history` says "add console inputs to the R history", and `repl_dispatch()` obeyed it, but the adds
  of Tasks 2 and 3 did not: the drained partial line, a line a question reads past, a pasted block and an invalid
  approval answer. In the pty with `gptr.history = FALSE` the approval typo `instead` and the unfinished
  `use base R` reached the history, though the REPL's own prompt did not.
- Red (`test '^console-(ui|repl|typing)$'`, the new tests on the old code): `[ FAIL 10 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 |
  PASS 503 ]`. `console_history_add()` returned NULL and added `a prompt` with the option off. In the drain test
  with the option off the history still got `use base R`, and no `[gptr] not kept (gptr.history is off): 'use base
  R'` row was shown. With the option off, the approval printed no `Not an answer: 'zz'. Type y, n or ?.` and the
  paste notice still read `(kept in history)`. The history mock got `zz` and `"""\na\nb\n"""`.
- Green: `console_history_add()` returns FALSE without adding when `gptr.history` is off, TRUE when it added, and
  FALSE when `utils::timestamp()` fails. The callers say `(kept in history)` only when it returned TRUE. The
  drained partial line is otherwise quoted as `[gptr] not kept (gptr.history is off): '<line>'`. `repl_dispatch()`
  and `/undo` (`ckpt_cmd_run()`, P16) call it without their own copy of the option check. The test helpers
  (`local_readline()`, `console_try()` and the paste test) now mock `utils::timestamp()`, not
  `console_history_add()`, so the real gate runs and the developer's history is never touched; the pty B, C, H
  test sets `gptr.history = TRUE`, since it recalls the kept line with Up.
  `test '^(console-(ui|repl|typing)|ckpt-rewind|ckpt-commands|console-commands)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 848 ]`.
- pty check (scratch driver, 80x24, scenario B with `gptr.history = FALSE`): the screen shows
  `  [gptr] not kept (gptr.history is off): 'use base R'`, then `  Not an answer: 'instead'. Type y, a, p, n or ?.`
  at the approval. Up at the next `peter> ` recalls R's own `source(...)` line, the newest entry: gptr added
  nothing.
- Also: 04 §3.1 (`gptr.history`), §7.1 (`console_history_add()`), §15.10 IC-75 and IC-76, and D-243's review note.
  `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`: `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 8 files, no lints. Neighbours with the layering and checkpoint tests
  (`^(console-|gptr-config|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|session-object|arch-layers|ckpt-)`): FAIL 0, WARN 0,
  SKIP 1, PASS 4752.
- Deviations: `/undo`'s history add now goes through `console_history_add()` too (one rule; it gains the `"""`
  block for a prompt of several lines).

### Fix 4 - An invalid approval answer gets the prompt's letters and the feedback form (Task 3)
- Findings: the hint `Not an answer: 'zz' (kept in history). Type y, a, p, n or ?.` named a `p` that

### Fix 5 - A /command or !code line a question reads past waits for the next prompt (Task 3)
- Finding: `console_typed_ahead()` queued every stale line as a steer, `/command` and `!code` lines too, while
  `console_typed()` keeps those in the reader for the next prompt (IC-76) and D-242 says a draft starting with `/` or
  `!` is never a steer. In RStudio, Positron and Windows consoles, where a question is the only route for a line
  typed during a run, and in a terminal between the drain and the question's `readline()`, `/exit` or `!rm(x)`
  reached the agent as an instruction.
- Red (`test '^console-ui$'`, the new test on the old code): `[ FAIL 4 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 141 ]`. A stale
  `!zz2 = 5` at an approval during a run left `zz2` unset, REQ2 held its relay, the screen showed
  `[gptr] steering: '!zz2 = 5'` and no `[gptr] after this answer: '!zz2 = 5'`.
- Green: the split of typed lines into held (`/`, `!`, continuing held code) and queued text, its two notices and
  the partial line's history add move from `console_typed()` into `console_typed_route(rd, s, lines, part)`.
  `console_typed()` reads fd 0 and calls it, then resumes the rendering when it returned TRUE (it paused). Now
  `console_typed_ahead()` calls it with the REPL's reader and resumes nothing, because the question or the pause
  menu owns the screen. Without a reader (no REPL) the line is still queued. `test
  '^(console-(ui|typing|interrupt)|arch-layers)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 1 | PASS 391 ]`.
- pty check (scratch driver, 80x24; pty D's trace of `ui_permission_lines()`, so the line arrives after the drain):
  `!zz2 = 5` typed then shows `  allow? [y]es / [a]lways / [n]o / [?]: !zz2 = 5`, then
  `  [gptr] after this answer: '!zz2 = 5'`, then the question again. `y` gives `REQ tool_result: ... END` with no
  relay, the next prompt shows `peter> !zz2 = 5` and runs it, and after `/exit` the child prints `ZZ2 = 5`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 3's decision names the `/command` and `!code` routing. Also: 04 §15.10 IC-75,
  03 §6.17, `?peter` (`R/console-repl.R`, `man/peter.Rd`, identical to a scratch `roxygenise()`), the
  interactive-console vignette (`.Rmd`, `.Rmd.orig`) and D-243's review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`:
  `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours with `arch-layers`: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4063.
- Deviations: the amendment above.

### Fix 6 - The turn_end hook only reads typed input (Task 7)
- Finding: the `policy_flush()` call in `console_on_turn_end()` did nothing. `console_typed()` queues through
  `policy_enqueue()`, which already flushes; items held while a tool ran are flushed by `console_on_tool_end()` at
  the `tool_execution_end` that `dispatch_finish()` emits outside the tool frame, before `turn_end`; with a tool
  frame still on the stack (a sub-agent's `turn_end` inside the parent's `r` tool) `policy_flush()` returns at once.
- Red: none. This removes code without changing behaviour, and no test depends on the call (the reviewer's
  scratch copy without it: `^(console-|agent-run|agent-loop|agent-dispatch|session-object)` FAIL 0, PASS 3463).
- Green: the hook body is `console_typed(); NULL`. `test '^console-typing$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS
  149 ]`, among them "a line typed during a tool reaches the request right after its result (D-242)", which keeps
  the periodic typing task from ticking.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 7's decision drops `policy_flush()`. Also: 04 §7.14 (`builtin_console` row)
  and D-242's Task 7 note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`: `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 1 file, no lints. Neighbours: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4019.
- Deviations: the amendment above.

### Fix 7 - The stale guard's descriptions match Task 3 (Tasks 2-3; docs only)
- Findings: `console_before_message()`'s doc still said the drain reads "the lines IC-75's stale guard would
  discard"; since Task 3 that guard queues them or keeps them in the history. The 03 §6.17 sentence listed
  `gptr_confirm()` among the console questions whose stale line is queued during a run, but `gptr_confirm()` keeps
  the default `keep` (the history), also during a run (`egress_check()` asks it inside a run for a nested call's
  provider), and `console_before_message()` does not drain before it, so IC-76's empty start does not hold for it.
- Red, green: none; comments and spec text only, no code changes.
- Changes: the doc says the drain reads "the lines typed for the run (routed as console_typed() routes them) and
  the partial line the question would take as its answer". 03 §6.17 says a console question routes its stale
  line during a run, while `gptr_confirm()`, and any question outside a run, puts it in the console history. The
  04 §15.10 IC-76 entry adds that `gptr_confirm()` (L0) is not drained. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py`:
  `All four plan checks passed`.
- Lint: 1 file, no lints.
- Deviations: none.

## Task 10 - The transcript is written in whole rows (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-(render|wait)$'`, the new and rewritten tests on HEAD's renderer):
  `[ FAIL 90 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 591 ]`. "the transcript is written in whole rows" failed 80 times (20 random
  chunkings in each of the four modes: a write ended inside a row). Its exact case wrote `  Hello` at the first
  write and ` world and` at the second, and after the wrap `  delta`, against nothing until the line end
  (`  Hello world and more\n`) and the wrap (`  alpha beta gamma\n`). `keeps streaming ` was written before
  `reset_line()`. A fence's first write gave `  ```r\n  x = mean(`, and at 256 colours the fenced line was
  redrawn (`y)\r  x = mean(y)`), the closing fence too. A fenced line broken by a notice showed `      y = foo(`
  before it, and `  if x:\r  if x:`. The waiting row stopped at the answer's first word (` y`), and
  test-console-wait.R's `# Hea` cleared it for a style alone (`\r<spaces>\r  \033[0;1;4m`).
- Green: `emit()` appends to the held row (`st$row`) and writes it only at a line end, so `before_output()` (which
  clears the waiting row) and `console_cat()` see whole rows; a wrap writes its `\n`, and `reset_line()` and
  `finish()` end the held row as before. `fence_stream()`, `fence_end()` and `st$shown` are deleted: a fence marker
  and a fenced line are each emitted once with their line end, so nothing is redrawn and a fenced line broken by
  other output is held whole until its end (one mode everywhere). The description of `render_markdown_stream()`
  and of `console_stream_output()` say so. `test '^console-(render|wait)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 681 ]`; `test '^console-(render|fences)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 1021 ]`, test-console-fences.R unchanged.
  - Two more expectations of the old timing, found by the first neighbour and CLI runs, are rewritten: the waiting
    row test of test-console-wait.R ("the first whole row ends the waiting row, not a style or a partial row") and
    test-cli-claude.R:854 (#31), whose fixture (`claude-toolstream.ndjson`) sent three deltas of one row 0.4 s
    apart; written whole, they arrived 0.00 s apart. The fixture now sends three rows (`Streamed first,\n`, ...).
- pty check (scratch driver, not kept: terminal R in a processx pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, temporary HOME,
  R_USER_* and GPTR_PROJECT_ROOT, `COLORFGBG=15;0`, en_US.UTF-8; `peter(model = fake, mode = "auto")`, then `go`;
  the fake answer is two prose rows at 80 columns, an r fence of two lines and `Done.`, sent 4 characters every
  0.06 s; the driver keeps each read of the pty, polled every 20 ms). This branch: 26 reads after `go`. The waiting
  row `  ▌ thinking (0s, Ctrl-C to steer or stop)` is redrawn 13 times, then cleared (`\r\033[K`) as the first row
  `  The model fits well. The slope on wt is negative and large enough to matter\r\n` arrives; every later read is
  whole rows ending in `\r\n`, the fence rows once each (`  fit = \033[1mlm\033[22m(mpg ~ wt, data = mtcars)\r\n`),
  and `  done · 1 request · 1.7k tokens · $0.0000` follows. No read leaves a half row on the bottom row and no bare
  CR follows the first answer row. HEAD (the same driver on `git archive HEAD`): 67 reads, 53 of which leave a half row
  on the bottom row (`  The`, `  The model`, `  The model fits well.`, ...), and 3 bare CRs (the fence redraws).
- Also: spec 04 §7.14 (`render_markdown_stream()` writes whole rows only; `reset_line()` and `finish()` end the
  held row; a fenced line is written once), 03 §6.17 (the Rendering item and the fenced-code sentence) and D-245's
  Task 10 note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set; without it the indexer finds no
  dependency package and reports 14121 errors, on HEAD too): `All four plan checks passed`, with `999 findings
  (info 826, warn 173)` against HEAD's `998 (info 826, warn 172)`. The new one is `prose_mentions_undefined` for
  `console_stream_output()` in P28 Task 10's Files line (an internal function no plan defines).

  once, after it) and the two waiting-row tests (the waiting row stays until the first whole row); the spec line

  flagged row) and the sub-agent's ask as `  sub-agent 'count' #1 > r  x = 1`; both read the prompt

## Task 14 - A follow-up is the next prompt (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-typing$'`, the new tests and the rewritten one on HEAD's code):
  `[ FAIL 12 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 163 ]`. With `type_ahead = "follow_up"` the typed `then plot it` was taken
  by run 1 when the agent would stop: REQ2's last message had source `follow_up`, and one status line was printed,
  not two. The batch `!x = 1`, `use x`, `!y = 2` sent `use x` inside run 1, before `!x = 1` ran, so REQ2 carried
  no `> x = 1` note and no `peter[auto]> use x` echo followed. The pause menu's [s] `!zz = 5` became a relay
  (REQ2 roles `user,assistant,operator`, last `The user sent this message while you were working: !zz = 5`),
  `zz` stayed unset, three requests were made and no `-> follow-up - !zz = 5` line was printed.
- Green: `test '^console-typing$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 176 ]`.
  - `policy_enqueue()` holds a follow-up, and any text starting with `/` or `!`, while a prompt of a REPL that is
    not piped runs (`console_repl_find()$sending`, not `.stdin` or `jsonl`): it prints the queue line
    (`console_queue_line()`, Task 13's line moved into a helper) and appends the text to the reader's `typed`
    lines; the `input` event comes when the text is sent. Its callers (the typing task, a line a question reads
    past, the pause menu) are unchanged, and outside such a REPL a follow-up still goes to the session's FIFO
    (test-console-interrupt.R's programmatic [f] case passes unchanged).
  - `console_typed_route()` appends the held lines typed before a batch's first text line, then the message, then
    the other held lines, so `!x = 1`, `use x`, `!y = 2` run in that order: REQ2's last message is `use x` (source
    `pipe`, as for any second prompt) carrying the `> x = 1` note and not `y = 2`; `x` and `y` are 1 and 2.
  - The reader echoes a typed entry with its line feeds kept (`console_escape(x)`), so a message of several lines
    shows its rows after `peter[auto]> `; a one-line `/command` or `!code` entry is echoed as before.
  - With `type_ahead = "follow_up"` and verbosity 2 the transcript reads `-> follow-up - then plot it` during run 1,
    its status line `  done - 1 request - ...`, then `peter[auto]> then plot it` and run 2's status line; REQ2's
    roles are `user,assistant,user`. No person's line `> then plot it` is printed: the run never takes it.
  - The pause menu's [s] `!zz = 5` and [f] `then plot it` (two simulated Ctrl-C in the REPL) print
    `-> follow-up - !zz = 5` and `-> follow-up - then plot it`; after run 1 the REPL runs `!zz = 5`, then sends
    `then plot it` as REQ2 (roles `user,assistant,user`, the `> zz = 5` note with it).
- pty check (scratch driver, not kept: terminal R in a processx pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color, en_US.UTF-8,
  `COLORFGBG=15;0`, temporary HOME, R_USER_* and GPTR_PROJECT_ROOT; `peter(model = fake, mode = "auto")` with
  `gptr.type_ahead = "follow_up"`; the fake streams 42 words for `go` and `again`, else echoes the last user text
  and the roles). `!x = 41` + Enter and `then use x` + Enter typed while `go` streams: the screen shows
  `  [gptr] after this answer: '!x = 41'` and `\033[2m↳ follow-up · then use x\033[22m` amid the answer, run 1's
  `  done · 1 request · ...`, then `peter[auto]> !x = 41`, `peter[auto]> then use x` and
  `  REQ2 last=then use x roles=user,assistant,user END` with its own status line. `again`, then Ctrl-C while it
  streams: `  [gptr] paused s...: [s]teer, [f]ollow-up, [c]ontinue, [a]bort,` / `  [b]ackground`, `  ? f`,
  `  follow-up> and plot it`, `↳ follow-up · and plot it`; the answer resumes to `END` and its status line, then
  `peter[auto]> and plot it` gives `REQ4 last=and plot it` as a run of its own. After `/exit` the child prints
  `X = 41`.
- Also: spec 04 §15.10 IC-76 (Task 14's item; Tasks 15-16 and 18-21 pending), 03 §6.2 (the REPL keeps its own
  follow-ups; outside it [f] uses the FIFO) and §6.17 (typed follow-ups are the next prompt, in the order typed with
  the held lines), D-242's Task 14 note, `?peter` (`R/console-repl.R`, `man/peter.Rd` regenerated by
  `isolated-check.R document`, which changed no other file), the follow-up sentences of
  vignettes/interactive-console.Rmd (the type-ahead paragraph and the pause menu's Follow-up) and the `type_ahead`
  row of vignettes/configuration.Rmd (and their `.orig`). `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER

- Plan amendment (same commit):

## Task 15 - Nothing queued is lost (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-(typing|interrupt)$'`, the new tests and the rewritten ones on HEAD's code):
  `[ FAIL 13 | WARN 0 | SKIP 1 | PASS 232 ]`. An abort while `/status` and `!x = 1` were held and `use base R`
  was queued added nothing to the history mock and printed `[gptr] dropped queued messages: 'use base R'` and
  `[gptr] dropped typed lines: '/status', '!x = 1'`; Ctrl-C at an approval and Ctrl-C [a] after a steer typed in
  a tool (`use mpg`) kept nothing either. After a 400 error the queued steer stayed in the FIFO and went out with
  the next prompt: REQ2's last message was `use base R`, not `next`. At `gptr.max_turns_console = 1` a steer typed
  while the first answer called r stayed queued and the REPL read `/exit`: one request, not two.
- Green: `test '^console-(typing|interrupt)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 1 | PASS 248 ]`.
  - `console_on_agent_end()` ends with `console_queue_left(rec, status)` for a foreground run (depth 0, not
    background; a tool may be on the stack, as at an approval asked inside one). After `idle` or `max_turns`, in a
    REPL whose prompt runs (`console_repl_prompt()`, the condition `policy_enqueue()` used, now one helper), the
    user steers still queued (`console_unqueue()`: `session_unqueue()` until NULL, oldest first) and the steers
    `policy_flush()` still holds for the session are appended to the reader's `typed` lines. The max_turns case
    prints `peter[auto]> use base R` and REQ2's roles are `user,assistant,tool_result,user`, the last
    `use base R`; the session's steer FIFO is empty.
  - After any other status nothing is sent. The user items the abort moved to `dropped` (counted from `rec$d0`,
    set by `console_track()`), the user steers still queued, the items held and the REPL's held lines go to
    `console_keep()`: each to `console_history_add()` in that order, then one notice. The abort case keeps
    `use base R`, `/status`, `!x = 1` and prints `[gptr] kept in history (Up): 'use base R', '/status',
    '!x = 1'` once, no `dropped`, one request, `x` unset; the error case keeps `use base R` and REQ2 ends with
    `next`; the approval case keeps `!x = 1`; the tool case keeps `use mpg` and the unrelated next run never sees
    it. The partial-line notice of `console_typed_route()` uses `console_keep()` too.
  - Deleted: `[gptr] dropped typed lines` (`console_send()`), `[gptr] dropped queued messages` (`policy_flush()`
    and `policy_abort()`), and the `run` of a held item. `policy_flush()` now always enqueues: an item of a run
    that ended first is settled by that run's `agent_end`, any other reaches its session's queue for its next run.
  - Follow-ups still in the session's FIFO (outside the REPL; also a session's queued first prompt) stay queued
    after an error. A first version took them too and turned test-console-jupyter-progress.R and
    test-console-render.R red (their synthetic `agent_end` with status `error` or `aborted` kept the session's
    unsent first prompt, `offline progress` and `hello`); the plan lists only steers from the FIFO.
  - The full suite then found one layering violation (test-arch-layers.R: `console_unqueue (console-render.R) ->
    session_unqueue (session-object.R)`): `session_unqueue()` is not in IC-33's kernel SDK. 04 §7.6 names P28 as
    its consumer, so `arch_contract_edges()` (helper-arch.R) adds the console edge, as for `model_canonical()`;
    `test '^arch-layers$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 44 ]`.
- pty check (scratch driver, not kept: terminal R in a processx pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color,
  `COLORFGBG=15;0`, temporary HOME, R_USER_* and GPTR_PROJECT_ROOT, gptr.history on;
  `peter(model = fake, mode = "auto")`, `go`; the fake streams 62 words in 8-character chunks 0.08 s apart).
  While it streams, `!x = 1` + Enter, then `use base R` + Enter, then Ctrl-C and `a` at the pause menu. In
  en_US.UTF-8 the rows read `  [gptr] after this answer: '!x = 1'`, `\033[2m↳ steer · use base R\033[22m`, the
  menu, `  aborted · 1 request · unknown tokens · unknown cost` (grey), then
  `  [gptr] kept in history (↑): 'use base R', '!x = 1'` and
  `  [gptr] aborted; the session is kept.`. Up at `peter[auto]> ` showed `!x = 1`, Up again `use base R`; after
  Ctrl-U and `/exit` the child printed `X IS unset` and `REQUESTS 1`. With LANG=C the same rows read
  `-> steer - use base R` and `kept in history (Up)`. HEAD (bbf10d8, from `git archive`) printed
  `[gptr] dropped queued messages: 'use base R'` and `[gptr] dropped typed lines: '!x = 1'`, and Up recalled `go`.
- Also: spec 04 §15.10 IC-76 (Task 15's item; Task 14's item now says the held lines are kept; Tasks 16 and 18-21
  pending), 03 §6.2 (nothing the person queued is lost; the pause menu writes to stdout through the console
  writer, fixing the doc drift that said stderr) and §6.17 (held lines go to the history after an interrupt),
  D-242's Task 15 note, `?peter` (`R/console-repl.R`, `man/peter.Rd` regenerated by `isolated-check.R document`,
  which changed no other file) and the type-ahead paragraph and Abort item of vignettes/interactive-console.Rmd (and
  its `.orig`). `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, with

  `contract_fn_undefined` for `console_queue_left()` and `console_unqueue()` in 04, as for earlier tasks' names.
- Plan amendment (same commit):
  - The draft: before the composer (Tasks 16-21) gptr holds no draft; a partial line stays in the terminal. Task 15
    says the draft joins the kept list once the composer holds it, and Task 20 adds that after [a] the draft joins
    it (`console_keep()`).
  - The rule is general: `idle` and `max_turns` hand steers on; any other end (an abort, an error, a block, the
    budget) keeps the text, as "nothing is auto-sent" asks. The leftovers are settled at `agent_end`
    (`console_queue_left()`), so one notice lists them, in the order dropped, still queued, held, the REPL's held
    lines. `idle` and `max_turns` steers are appended after the held lines (they become follow-ups then).
    Outside the REPL they stay queued for the session's next run, and FIFO follow-ups stay after an error.
  - Tests: the error case and the two rewritten cases are named. Spec adds 03 §6.17. Files names
    `policy_abort()`, `console_repl_prompt()`, `console_queue_left()`, `console_send()`, `console_keep()`, `?peter`,
    helper-arch.R (the contract edge, also stated in 04 §15.10) and the vignette. File Structure: the
    console-render.R row adds 15 (the original Task 15 Files line named it), and a helper-arch.R row is added.

## Review fixes of Tasks 8-15 (2026-10-21)
A review of Tasks 8-15 confirmed 14 findings (two of them the same defect). Each fix below is its own commit. The
pty checks run the review's scratch driver (not kept: terminal R in a pty, 80x24, TERM=xterm-256color,
`COLORFGBG=15;0`, en_US.UTF-8, temporary HOME, R_USER_* and GPTR_PROJECT_ROOT, with a VT emulator that keeps SGR)
on its scenarios s1-s9.

### Fix 1 - A line queued while an answer streams goes between its whole rows (Tasks 10, 13)
- Finding: `console_typed_route()` paused the rendering for a typed steer: `md$finish()` wrote the held row as a
  short row and ended the Markdown block, `rec$last = NULL` put a blank line after the queue line, and the resume
  started a new waiting row, `▌ thinking (0s, ...)`, inside the answer still streaming. Two steers cut one paragraph
  into three blocks; TERM=dumb showed the same split.
- Red (`test '^console-(typing|render)$'`, the new and rewritten tests on HEAD's code):
  `[ FAIL 4 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 798 ]`. 40 words streamed at 40 columns with `use base R` typed after four
  looks gave `  word11 word12 word13 word14`, the queue line, a blank row, a `# thinking (0s, ...)` row, then
  `  word15 ...`, against the eight rows of the whole answer around the queue line. A question asked while
  `partial ` was held wrote `partial` before the call line, and the evaluation case wrote `Fanning out` before
  the sub-agent's call line.
- Green: `console_typed_route()` clears only the foreground records' waiting rows
  (`console_before_message(kind = NULL, tools = TRUE)`, the spinner keeps its clock and task) instead of
  `console_render_pause()`, and returns nothing; `console_typed()` resumes nothing. `render_markdown_stream()` loses
  `reset_line()` (nothing of the held row is on screen, so there is nothing to end), as does the CLI thinking line,
  whose `reset_line()` did nothing; `console_before_message()` only clears waiting rows. Rewritten: the render tests
  "other output goes between whole rows; the held row continues, nesting kept (D-245)" (two notes between writes:
  `  keeps streaming slowly on.` is one row), the fenced line broken by a notice (no `reset_line()` call, same rows),
  "the console UI prompts between whole rows; the held row waits (D-186, D-245)", the evaluation case (the held row
  `Fanning out HELDWORD` follows the question, written by the pause); deleted: "reset_line() ends a partial line"
  and test-console-typing.R's "the held-lines notice ends the streamed line first (render pause)".
  `test '^console-(typing|render)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 802 ]`.

### Fix 2 - Held /command and !code lines show as follow-ups (Tasks 13, 14; two findings)
- Findings: a typed `/command` or `!code` line held for the next prompt still printed
  `  [gptr] after this answer: '!x = 1'` (`console_typed_route()`), while a follow-up typed in the same batch and a
  `!code` line given at the pause menu printed Task 13's `↳ follow-up · ...`, though all three wait in
  `rd$typed` and run as the next prompts (pty s3: the notice, `↳ follow-up · use x`, the notice again).
- Red (`test '^console-(typing|ui)$'`, the new expectations on HEAD's code): `[ FAIL 7 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 |
  PASS 355 ]`. No `-> follow-up - /status`, `-> follow-up - !x = 1`, `-> follow-up - !zz2 = 5` (a line a
  question reads past) or `-> follow-up - !f = function(x) {` row; `after this answer` still printed; the three
  lines of the follow-up batch `!x = 1`, `use x`, `!y = 2` were not all queue lines in that order.
- Green: `console_typed_route()` prints `console_queue_line(s, <entry>, "follow_up")` for each held entry, in the
  order typed: the held lines before the batch's message, the message (its own queue line from
  `policy_enqueue()`), then the others. An entry is a `/` or `!` line with the lines that continue it while its code
  is incomplete (`typed_code_open()`, now computed for each line as `cont`), so `!f = function(x) {` and `  x + 1`
  show as one queue line, the later row under the text. The notice is deleted. `test '^console-(typing|ui)$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 362 ]`.
- pty check (s3: `type_ahead = "follow_up"`; `!x = 1`, `use x` and `!y = 2` typed while 70 words stream). Rows:
  `↳ follow-up · !x = 1`, `↳ follow-up · use x` and `↳ follow-up · !y = 2` between the answer's whole rows, all
  dim; after the status line `peter[auto]> !x = 1`, `peter[auto]> use x` (its own run, `REQ3 user: use x`) and
  `peter[auto]> !y = 2`.
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-75 (the routing sentence) and IC-76 (Task 14's item), 03 §6.17, D-242's review note.
  `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1017 findings`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 14 says the held `/cmd` and `!code` lines print the follow-up's queue line,

### Fix 3 - Outside an interactive R nothing queued is settled (Task 15)
- Finding: `console_queue_left()` ran at the end of every foreground run, also under Rscript. With a timer calling
  `gptr_steer(s, "please also check units")` while a fake 400 response was pending, the end of the run took the
  steer off the queue (`console_unqueue()`) and printed `[gptr] kept in history (↑): 'please also check units'`,
  but `utils::timestamp()` does nothing outside an interactive R: the steer was lost while the notice said it was
  kept. Before Task 15 it stayed queued for the session's next run. An abort under Rscript listed its dropped items
  the same way.
- Red (`test '^console-(typing|interrupt)$'`, the new test on HEAD's code): `[ FAIL 3 | WARN 0 | SKIP 1 |
  PASS 253 ]`. The steer queue was empty against `please also check units`, the history mock got the steer and the
  output held `kept in history`.
- Green: `console_queue_left()` starts with `if (!gptr_is_interactive()) return(invisible(NULL))`. The Task 15
  tests (the abort, error, max_turns and approval cases in test-console-typing.R, the tool case in
  test-console-interrupt.R) mock `gptr_is_interactive()` TRUE; the REPL then adds its own inputs to the history
  too, so their history mocks read `go`, the kept items, then `next` or `/exit`. `test
  '^console-(typing|interrupt)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 1 | PASS 256 ]`.
- Rscript check (scratch script, not kept: `Rscript --vanilla`, verbosity 2, `peter("go")`, then
  `s |> peter("next")` against a 400 answered after 1.5 s, with `gptr_steer(s, "please also check units")` from a
  reactor timer 0.8 s in). HEAD (0ee69a0, from `git archive`): `[gptr] kept in history (↑): 'please also check
  units'` and `STEERS QUEUED: 0`. This commit: no notice and `STEERS QUEUED: 1`.
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-76 (Task 15's item), 03 §6.2, D-242's review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with
  R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1017 findings`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 15 says the rule holds in an interactive R only.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4427.
- Deviations: the amendment above.

- Finding: `console_queue_left()` runs inside `run_abort()` (the run's `agent_end`), so the kept list printed after

### Fix 5 - The pause menu's notice check matches the old notice (Task 13; test only)
- Finding: Task 13's check `expect_false(any(grepl("[gptr] steering|follow-up queued", err)))` in "steer and

- Finding: `  ? Allow edit?  y yes · a always (session) · p always (project) · n no · ? more` is 80 columns against a
  width of 78, so the line wrapped for every tool name of 4 characters or more (write, edit and MCP names: 87-96
  columns) and every sub-agent ask. `console_wrap()` kept the 2-column lead: the first row ended in a dangling ` ·`
  and the second, `  ? more`, put the bold `?` key under the blue question `?`, where it read as a second question
  (pty s1, s4).

  WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 986 ]`: every question still carried its keys on its own row, `  ? Allow r?  y yes - n no -
  ? more`, and `  ? sub-agent a: allow r?  y yes - a always (session) - p always (project) -` over
  `  n no - ? more` in the piped `.stdin` case.
- Green: `ui_permission_lines()` ends with `  ? <question>` and, under its text, `    <keys>` wrapped at the rows'
  width (continuing at column 4). At 80 columns: `  ? Allow r?` / `    y yes · a always (session) · p always
  (project) · n no · ? more`; `  ? sub-agent 'Summarise the data' #12: allow edit?` over the same 68-column row; at
  50 columns in the margin `    y yes · a always (session) ·` / `    p always (project) · n no · ? more`. At 256
  colours the question is `  \033[38;5;67m?\033[39m \033[1mAllow r?\033[22m` and the keys row
  `    \033[1my\033[22m\033[2m yes · \033[22m...`. Rewritten: the question expectations of test-console-ui.R,
  test-console-render.R and test-console-repl.R, and the flagged case's row count (9). `test
  '^console-(ui|render|repl)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 1002 ]`.
- pty check (s1, s4: manual mode, an r fence call, an edit, a sub-agent's ask). Each ask reads `  ? Allow r?`,
  `  ? Allow edit?` or `  ? sub-agent 'count the rows of mtcars' #1: allow r?` over
  `    y yes · a always (session) · p always (project) · n no · ? more`, then `› ` at column 0.
- Kept: the no-break spaces. The review said they could go, but a console narrower than 72 columns (an RStudio
  pane) still wraps the keys row, and without them a key letter could end a row apart from its words.

- Finding: read calls were grouped or spaced by whether the model sent them in one response, which the person
  cannot see. The waiting row between requests opened with `console_gap(rec, "wait")`, a blank line after a tool
  block (pty s6: `▎ read  a.R`, a blank row, `▎ read  b.R`, a blank row, `▎ read  R/f.R` directly over
  `▎ r  nrow(mtcars)`, which came in one response).

## Task 16 - The composer's key machine (2026-10-21)
- Red (`test '^console-input$'`, the new file on HEAD's code): `[ FAIL 8 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 0 ]`; every block
  stopped at `could not find function "console_compose"`.
- Green: `console_compose(state, chars, now, enter_kind, complete)` and `console_trigger(line)` in the new
  `R/console-input.R`, pure; nothing calls them before Task 18. `test '^console-input$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 23 ]`, in en_US.UTF-8 and under `LC_ALL=C`.
  - A batch is split into escape sequences (`ESC [` parameters final, `ESC O` x, `ESC` x), single control bytes and
    runs of text. The edit keys act on the text after the last newline, and the text up to it is one message.
    `plot 数据` + DEL gives `plot 数`, + Ctrl-W `plot `; `ab ESC[D c` gives `abc`; `x = 1\r\ny = 2\rz` sends
    `x = 1\ny = 2` and keeps `z`; `abc\n` + DEL in one batch sends `abc`, and the DEL edits nothing.
  - Escapes: a lone ESC at 0 s is held (`esc`), `[D` at 0.01 s completes it and both are dropped, `[1;` at 0.04 s
    keeps the start time 0, then `5C` drops the whole sequence; at 0.06 s the ESC is gone, so `d` is text (`abcd`)
    and `[A` is text, not Up.
  - Tab: `use @mtc` gives `use @mtcars` (the common prefix of `@mtcars` and `@mtcars_copy`), a second Tab
    `complete(c("@mtcars", "@mtcars_copy"))`; `!!summary(mtcars_c` gives `!!summary(mtcars_copy`; `use @zz`,
    `/status` and `!zz` (no match) do nothing; `summarise the da`, `mail a@b.com`, `cost is $5`, `use $name`, `x$y`
    and `wow! x` go as the other kind; `/model gpt` + Tab is a follow-up with either `type_ahead`.
  - The first version of the test had two expectations per case, and in CRAN mode its Tab block took 110 ms
    (D-240 skips blocks of 25 ms or more on CRAN). One `expect_identical()` per table, whose difference names the
    case (`actual$use @mtc + \t[[1]]`), brings every block under 16 ms warm, so none needs `skip_on_cran()`.
- Layering: `test '^arch-layers$'` with the new file gave `[ FAIL 2 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 42 ]` (`console-input.R`
  not in the layer table, and `console_compose (console-input.R) -> %||% (aaa-state.R)`). helper-arch.R adds
  `console-input.R`, L5, P28; test-arch-layers.R expects 123 rows; 03 §3.2 adds the row (123 files). Then
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 44 ]`.
- CRAN list: `console-input` joins `tests/testthat.R` (66 files; D-240 says so).
- Plan amendment (same commit), where Task 16 was underspecified:
  - At most one action, not a list: a read batch sends at most one message.
  - `console_trigger()` holds the trigger rule, which Task 22's `console_complete()` reuses (Task 22 amended), so
    Tab never queues a trigger word even before completion exists; `complete(draft, nchar(draft))` returns full
    words, and their longest common prefix replaces the word, else two or more are listed.
  - `\r\n` and `\r` read as `\n`; a newline ends what DEL, Ctrl-U and Ctrl-W reach, so keys typed after Enter in
    one 50 ms poll never edit the sent text; Tab and Up act only as a batch of their own (elsewhere Tab is text,
    Up dropped); a blank message is not sent.
  - Spec and Files add 03 §3.2, helper-arch.R and test-arch-layers.R (the layer row), also for Task 22's new file;
    the File Structure row of helper-arch.R adds 16 and 22.
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-76 (Task 16's item; Tasks 18-21 pending), D-243's Task 16 note, D-240's file count.
  `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1023 findings (info
  826, warn 197)` against HEAD's 1020: `contract_fn_undefined` for `console_compose()` and `console_trigger()` in 04
  and `prose_mentions_undefined` for `console_trigger()` in P28, as for earlier tasks' names.
- Visual check (the capture toolkit in the session scratchpad, `vis/`, not kept). This commit's tree went to
  `vis/src-t16`, installed into `vis/lib-t16`, and the shared scenario ran as tag `t16`: a new Terminal.app window
  at 80x24 (window 520249, visible share 1.0, no stale capture; closed after), tmux at 60x20 and 80x24, and headless
  `t-dark`, `t-light`, `nocolor` (NO_COLOR) and `dumb` (TERM=dumb). Task 16 adds no call to the console, so no screen
  should change, and none did:
  - every run complete, no residue; the report's text groups are those of HEAD's capture (`runs/head-behaviour`,
    cfcd125): Terminal.app, tmux 80x24, `t-dark` and `t-light` one text, `nocolor` and `dumb` another (backticks
    and `-` bullets without colour); against HEAD's texts only timing differs, the row the queue line lands on and
    where Ctrl-C cut the answer; tmux 60x20 is identical.
  - Looked at: `vis/shots/terminals/t16/app-default-80x24/04-steer-draft.png`, `08-approval-ask.png` and
    `22-complete-at.png`; `vis/shots/terminals/t16/hl-nocolor-80x24/08-approval-ask-light.png`;

columns), VS Code's terminal, a fresh RStudio (light and Tomorrow Night), Positron, R.app and the degraded modes
(NO_COLOR, TERM=dumb, 8 and 256 colours, `cli.unicode = FALSE`, LANG=C, piped stdin, Rscript), and filed 38

  scenario ran as tag `pre`: four Terminal.app windows (default 80x24 and 120x40, Novel, Pro; each a new window
  captured by its id and closed after), tmux at 60x20, 80x24 and 120x40, every headless mode, then a fresh
  RStudio (light, Tomorrow Night), VS Code, Positron and R.app, each a new instance closed after. Each finding was
  checked on these screenshots before it was fixed, deferred or rejected.

  `use more fuel`; `summary()` and `head()` rows at column 0 in ink; the fence rows; the ink waiting row),

  and pads only where EL is unknown, R.app and Rgui (findings 15, 29: about 57 trailing spaces on ten RStudio
  rows), and ends `· ctrl-c pauses` in Positron (finding 20 gives it the menu).

  class, `  ? Paused` over a keys row built as the approval's, no session id unless several sessions are paused,
  no `[gptr] continuing`, answered at `› ` at column 0, [s] and [f] at the same chevron (findings 3, 31). The
  held answer row is fixed now (finding 21, below).

  (findings 10, 37: `$0.0000`, `aborted · 1 request · unknown tokens · unknown cost`); the banner names the
  console's own key and promises no pause where none is offered (finding 22: `Esc pauses an answer` in RStudio,
  where Esc stops the run). Its three dim rows at column 2 already answer finding 9.
- Task 28: scenario S10, Ctrl-C under load (finding 18: 5 of 22 runs at load average about 300 lost the ^C on
  0ee69a0; none of 26 at normal load, so the cause is unknown). Fix only once it fails; the fallback is Task 18's
  character mode with `-isig` while no tool runs. Not reproduced here: a soak beside 2 x nproc busy processes

### Fix 1 - The one grey is a colour where 256 colours show (findings 1, 28)
- Finding: the quiet tier was SGR 2, which RStudio maps to `filter: brightness(0.75)` (no change on black text) and
  Positron draws in full ink, so the approval's key words, the hints, fences, `↳` lines, read calls and (since the
  review of Tasks 8-15) the status line were as loud as the answer there.
- Red (`test '^console-(render|ui|typing)$'`, the 256-colour expectations rewritten to the grey on HEAD's code):
  `[ FAIL 33 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 974 ]`: `st$dim("x")` was `\033[2mx\033[22m` at every depth, link URLs
  `\033[0;2m`, quote marks, rules, fence lines and R comments `\033[2m`, the approval's keys row, flagged rows, hint,
  remembered line and queue lines `\033[2m`.
- Green: `console_style()` takes `grey = if (deep) "#878787" else "dim"` beside red and blue: `dim` is
  `cli::make_ansi_style(grey)` and the code theme's comment colour is `grey`. `#878787` is halfway between the
  logo's ink `#1c1e22` and cream `#f3f1ec`: cli's cell 244 at 256 colours (`38;5;244`), `38;2;135;135;135` in
  truecolor, 3.6:1 on white and 4.6:1 on the ink; SGR 2 stays below 256 colours. The renderer takes the grey's SGR
  parameters for link URLs as it does the blue's for inline code, and writes rules, quote marks and fence lines
  with `sym$dim()`. `test '^console-(render|ui|typing|fences)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 1425 ]` (two
  test corrections on the way: a rule is at least 20 columns wide, and the compaction case runs at 8 colours, so
  it keeps SGR 2).
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-82 (the palette sentence and the `dim` row), D-245's visual review note. `python3 -I
  dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1025 findings (info 827, warn
  198)`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 8 names the grey; Task 9's comments take it.
- Lint: 4 files, no lints. Neighbours: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4487.

### Fix 8 - Prose wraps at 100 columns at most (finding 17)
- Finding: answers wrapped at the width less 4, so in a maximised VS Code panel (131 columns,
  `pre-vscode/14-long-answer-streaming.png`) prose ran to about 127 characters a row, hard to read and much wider
  than the code and tool blocks around it.
- Red (`test '^console-render$'`, the new test at 140 columns, with and without the margin, on the previous
  commit's code): `[ FAIL 4 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 631 ]`: the first row held 19 words (132 columns of text).
- Green: `render_markdown_stream()`'s `set_width()` caps the measure at 100: `max(20L, min(w - 2L * nchar(pad),
  100L))`, one constant; code rows are not wrapped by the stream, a table wider than 100 prints verbatim, a rule is
  100 dashes. The first row now holds 14 words. Consoles of 104 columns or more now show the same rows.

### Fix 10 - Positron's console is an L console with the pause menu (finding 20)
- Finding: under ark `.Platform$GUI` is `X11` and stdin is a tty, so the guard `ide = human && !stdin_tty()` hid
  `POSITRON=1` and `TERM_PROGRAM=vscode` won: `front_end()` said `vscode` and Positron got the terminal class (an
  fd-0 typing task, stty on ark's tty, terminal hints). What the person typed during a run piled up in Positron's
  own input and ran as R code after `peter()` returned (`positron/99-final.png`: a `c` printed `.Primitive("c")`).
- Checked: Positron's ark binary (`/Applications/Positron.app/.../positron-r/resources/ark/ark`) holds
  `attach(list(), name = "tools:positron")`, so the console is known by that environment, while R in Positron's
  terminal tab has none.
- Red (`test '^(utils-options|console-render|console-interrupt)$'`, the new and extended tests on the previous
  commit's code): FAIL 4: `front_end()` was `vscode` with `tools:positron` attached, `repl_terminal()` was TRUE
  and `console_menu_available()` FALSE under a mocked `positron` front end, and the key was `Esc`.
- Green: `front_end()` returns `positron` when `tools:positron` is on the search path; `repl_terminal()` adds
  `front_end() != "positron"`; `console_menu_available()` offers the menu there (the reviewers' Positron checks
  resumed it); `console_interrupt_key()` names Ctrl-C, which is what reached R. `test
  '^(utils-options|console-render|console-interrupt|console-spinner-gui|console-spinner-width)$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN
  0 | SKIP 1 | PASS 842 ]`. `devtools::document()` (isolated) leaves `man/peter.Rd` as edited.
- Also: 04 §5 (`front_end()`) and IC-76's scope, `?peter` and the interactive-console vignette (and `.orig`): Ctrl-C
  in Positron's console, Esc in RStudio, R.app and RGui; D-243's visual review note. `python3 -I
  dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1026 findings`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): the class table says `repl_terminal()` excludes Positron.

### Fix 11 - An approval's invalid-answer hint names the question (finding 19, in part)
- Finding: in RStudio and R.app a draft typed during a run stays on the IDE's input line, so when the approval
  appeared the person's `y` became `and label the axesy`, an invalid answer; after a few notices the question had
  scrolled away, and every later line (an empty Enter, `1`, the next prompt) was read as another try until the
  call was refused (`light-faithful/24-prompt-c-sent.png`; the faithful run types blind).
- Red (`test '^console-ui$'`, the hint expectations rewritten and a sub-agent case added, on the previous commit's
  code): `[ FAIL 5 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 176 ]`: the hint was `  Type y, a, p or n (n <text> says what to do
  instead), or ?.`.
- Green: `ui_permission_question(request)` gives `Allow r?` (`sub-agent a: allow r?`), used by the question row
  and the hint: `  Allow r? Type y, a, p or n (n <text> says what to do instead), or ?.`. `test '^console-ui$'`:
  `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 181 ]`.
- Not taken from this finding, with reasons: treating an answer that ends in an offered key after other text as a
  leftover draft (and not counting it) would misread `okay` and `maybe`, which end in `y`, and the notice already
  quotes the whole line; not counting empty answers would let a question that reads only empty lines run forever.
  A person who sees the quoted line and the named question answers again.
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-77, D-243's visual review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with R_LIBS_USER set):
  `All four plan checks passed`, `1026 findings`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 3's hint.
- Lint: 2 files, no lints. Neighbours with the sub-agent tests: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 4854.
- Deviations: the amendment above.

### Fix 15 - A queue line waits for the answer's block and is a block of its own (findings 5, 26)
- Finding: `↳ steer · use base graphics only, no ggplot2` was written between two rows of one sentence (`Heavier
  cars should` / `↳ ...` / `use more fuel`, `05-steer-queued.png`), at a row that depended on when Enter was
  pressed; written during the waiting row it stuck to the next block with no blank row (VS Code: directly above
  `Understood: ...`). Writing whole rows (Task 10) prevents mid-row cuts, not mid-sentence ones, and Task 18
  moves the line to the live row only in T class, so TERM=dumb, ESS and the IDE consoles keep it.
- Red (`test '^console-typing$'`, the streaming test rewritten and a new waiting-row case, on the previous commit's
  code): `[ FAIL 3 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 199 ]`: the queue line sat after `word10` inside the paragraph, the
  answer was followed by the person's line alone, and `  first` came directly under the queue line.
- Green: `console_queue_line()` finds the session's foreground record; while its text block is open
  (`rec$md` set and `rec$last == "text"`) the rows wait in `rec$queued`, else they are written after
  `console_gap(rec, "person")`. `console_gap()` writes held rows first, as the person's block, when any block
  other than text starts (the waiting row, a tool call, the delivered `›` line, a notice, the status line), and
  joins a question and the person's lines read for it (`prompt person`), so the drain's lines stay right under the
  keys row (Fix 13). The answer reads `... trend in the scatter.`, a blank row, `↳ steer · use base R`, then the
  delivered `› use base R` as one block. `test '^console-typing$'`: `[ FAIL 0 | WARN 0 | SKIP 0 | PASS 202 ]`,
  with the pty case's text check collapsing the space left where it removes the queue line.
- Also: 04 §15.10 IC-82 (the whole-rows item), D-245's visual review note. `python3 -I dev/ci/check-plans.py` (with
  R_LIBS_USER set): `All four plan checks passed`, `1027 findings`.
- Plan amendment (same commit): Task 10's other-output decision.
- Lint: 3 files, no lints. Neighbours with the CLI tests: FAIL 0, WARN 0, SKIP 1, PASS 5445.
